# Point-set topology checklist (master)

One row per mathematical result of Morris, *Topology Without Tears* (2024). Statements are one-line paraphrases in our own words;
item references are `kind number @p<pdf page>` (pdf page = page index + 1 of the text in the refs). Book maps are generated from this
file by `scripts/coverage/book_maps.py` (never edit them by hand). Classification vocabulary: in Mathlib; partly in Mathlib;
proved here; not formalized; not a result. Names in `where` are checked mechanically against the Mathlib sources by
`scripts/coverage/check_topology.py --names`. Mathlib snapshot df6966d (2026-11-07).

## Chapter 3: closure, interior, neighbourhoods, connectedness basics

| id | statement | Morris (morris2024) | classification | where | note | subtopic |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TP-037 | A point x is a limit point (accumulation point) of A if every open set containing x meets A in a point other than x. | Def 3.1.1 @p73 | in Mathlib | AccPt; derivedSet; accPt_iff_nhds; mem_derivedSet | AccPt x (𝓟 A) via filter 𝓝[≠] x; derivedSet A is the set A'; accPt_iff_nhds gives the neighbourhood form. / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_1 | Limit points and closure |
| TP-038 | A subset is closed iff it contains all its limit points. | Prop 3.1.6 @p76 | in Mathlib | isClosed_iff_accPt; isClosed_iff_derivedSet_subset | Exact statement in terms of AccPt and derivedSet. / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_6 | Limit points and closure |
| TP-039 | For any A, the set A together with its limit points is closed. | Prop 3.1.8 @p77 | in Mathlib | closure_eq_self_union_derivedSet; isClosed_closure | Follows by rewriting closure A = A ∪ derivedSet A and closure is closed; no standalone lemma with this exact shape. / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_8 | Limit points and closure |
| TP-040 | The closure of A is defined as A together with its limit points. | Def 3.1.9 @p77 | in Mathlib | closure; closure_eq_self_union_derivedSet | Mathlib defines closure as the intersection of closed supersets; equality with A ∪ derivedSet A is proved. / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_9 | Limit points and closure |
| TP-041 | The closure of A is the smallest closed set containing A, i.e. the intersection of all closed supersets. | Rem 3.1.10 @p77 | in Mathlib | closure_minimal; subset_closure; isClosed_closure; closure | Mathlib's definition of closure (sInter of closed supersets); closure_minimal gives minimality. / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_10 | Limit points and closure |
| TP-042 | The rationals are dense in the reals, i.e. the closure of Q in R is R. | Ex 3.1.12 @p78 | in Mathlib | Rat.denseRange_cast; Rat.isDenseEmbedding_coe_real | Rat.denseRange_cast holds in any archimedean linear ordered field; real case via isDenseEmbedding_coe_real (Topology/Instances/Rat.lean). / course layer (this library): UndergradKB.Topology.Course.morris_3_1_12 | Limit points and closure |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| TP-093 | Brouwer fixed point theorem: a continuous self-map of a closed n-cube has a fixed point. | Rem 5.2.12 @p120 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.hasFixedPointProp_cube; UndergradKB.Topology.hasFixedPointProp_cell; UndergradKB.Topology.brouwer_closedBall; UndergradKB.Topology.no_retraction_cell; UndergradKB.Topology.no_contDiff_retraction; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_contDiff_fixedPointFree; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_contDiff_retraction_of_fixedPointFree; UndergradKB.Topology.brouwer_of_no_retraction; UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_cube_cell; UndergradKB.Topology.HasFixedPointProp.of_homeomorph | Searched Brouwer in Mathlib; only Brouwer algebras (order theory), no topological theorem. / this library: BrouwerVolume.lean, BrouwerSmooth.lean, Brouwer.lean: Brouwer's theorem proved for the n-cube (Fin n → unitInterval), for the closed unit ball of R^n and for the closed unit ball of any finite-dimensional real inner product space; no continuous retraction of the ball onto the sphere. Proof: a fixed-point-free continuous self-map is replaced by a smooth one (Stone-Weierstrass on coordinates, then shrinking); the ray retraction is then C^1 on an open set containing the ball; for small t the map id + t(r - id) is injective and maps the open ball onto itself (inverse function theorem, connectedness), so the change of variables formula gives vol(ball) = integral of det(1 + t(Dr - 1)), a polynomial in t of degree at most n (Lagrange interpolation), hence also at t = 1, where det Dr = 0 since ‖r‖ = 1. | Intermediate value theorem |

The "if" direction: a compact locally connected metric space is uniformly locally path connected (nested chains of small connected sets, limit of a recursive walk; Mazurkiewicz-Moore-Menger), then a continuous map from the Cantor set (Hausdorff-Alexandroff, Mathlib) is extended over the gaps by paths of nearly minimal radius. | Space-filling curves |
| TP-236 | Anderson-Bessaga-Kadec-Pelczynski-Torunczyk: every separable infinite-dimensional Frechet space is homeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{\aleph_0}$, and (second part) every infinite-dimensional Frechet space is homeomorphic to a Hilbert space. | Thm 9.6.1 @p257 | not formalized | - | Searched Bessaga, Kadec, Pelczynski, Torunczyk, Fréchet-space homeomorphism in Mathlib; not found. | Postscript |
| TP-237 | Every separable infinite-dimensional Banach space is homeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{\aleph_0}$, to $\ell^2$, and any two such spaces are homeomorphic. | Cor 9.6.2 @p257 · Cor 9.6.3 @p257 · Cor 9.6.4 @p257 | not formalized | - | Consequences of the previous row; no Mathlib statement found. | Postscript |
| TP-238 | Bessaga-Pelczynski: in an infinite-dimensional Banach space the whole space, a sphere and a closed ball are homeomorphic. | Thm 9.6.5 @p257 · Cor 9.6.7 @p258 | not formalized | - | Not found; Mathlib has only the easy open ball homeomorphic to space (Remark 9.6.6 style: Homeomorph.unitBall for normed spaces, not re-checked). | Postscript |
| TP-239 | For separable infinite-dimensional Frechet or Banach spaces, their balls, spheres, convex opens and countable powers (finite or infinite) are all homeomorphic. | Cor 9.6.8 @p258 | not formalized | - | Consequence of the Bessaga-Pelczynski and ABKPT theorems; not found. | Postscript |

Route: weak-* ball metrizable compact, Cantor set onto it, C(K) → C(Cantor) isometric, then linear interpolation over the omitted intervals C(Cantor) → C([0,1]) (real-valued, so no convexity of the ball is needed). NOTE: BanachMazur.lean imports BanachEmbedding, so it could only be checked in pieces (the .olean of BanachEmbedding was not built): everything except the last theorem compiled without the import, and the glue theorem banach_mazur compiled in a scratch copy with the lemma stubbed; the lead should confirm with a full build | Universal spaces |

Every separable metric space embeds isometrically in C(unitInterval, R) (Kuratowski into l^infty(N), then Banach-Mazur on the closed linear span of the image) and in C(N → unitInterval, R) (compose with the first projection); C(N → unitInterval, R) is separable. Missing for uncountable aleph: an isometric (e.g. linear norm-one) extension operator C(K) → C([0,1]^aleph) for K a closed subset of the cube of weight <= aleph (K = weak-* dual ball of the closed span of the Kuratowski image, which has density <= aleph); norm-preserving Tietze is not additive and the cube is non-metrizable, so no McShane formula; also the bound density(C([0,1]^aleph)) <= aleph (Stone-Weierstrass with an aleph-generated algebra) | Universal spaces |

Proof: Stefan lemma (stefan_periods: a point of minimal odd period m > 1 gives periods 1, all even k, all k > m): list the cycle increasingly, Markov graph of the m-1 gaps (SortedOrbit.Cov), loop gap, a second gap covering it (an odd cycle cannot alternate sides), reachability of all gaps (the orbit is one cycle), Hamiltonian chain of gaps without forward shortcuts (exists_hamiltonian_chain: no primitive odd closed walk of length 3..m-2 forces every simple cycle through the loop gap to have length m-1), spiral structure of the orbit (SpiralData.inv_all), the last gap covers all even-indexed gaps (SpiralData.final), then covering loops of gaps give periodic points of exact period k (exact period, or a cycle point, excluded by the period m or by three cycle points in one gap). General n = 2^a*m by induction on a through f^[2]. The proof is the standard Markov-graph one, not the book's (the book gives none). | Sarkovskii theorem |

New: the Stefan map stefanMap n (continuous, piecewise linear, values in [0, 2n], affine on every gap [g, g+1], one cycle n -> n+1 -> n-1 -> n+2 -> ... -> 0 -> n of prime period 2n+1, all nodes have that period) and not_odd_period_stefan (no point of odd prime period l with 3 <= l < 2n+1: a periodic orbit avoiding the nodes follows a closed walk of the Markov graph of the gaps, which for odd length l <= 2n-1 must stay at the loop gap, where the map is x -> 3n+1-2x with only the fixed point); with dblDataS_iterate (SharkovskiiConverse.lean) this gives 2^k*(2n+1) for all k, n >= 1; n = 2^k (and n = 2^k*3) were already in SharkovskiiConverse.lean. | Sarkovskii theorem |

| TP-491 | Properties of compact abelian G via the dual: weight equals the cardinal of the dual, metrizable iff dual countable, connected iff dual torsion-free, torsion-free iff dual divisible | Prop A5.0.18 @p504 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.topWeight_eq_card_dual; UndergradKB.Topology.topWeight; UndergradKB.Topology.topWeight_eq_card_dual_of_infinite; UndergradKB.Topology.card_dual_le_of_isTopologicalBasis; UndergradKB.Topology.topWeight_pi_circle_le; UndergradKB.Topology.card_dual_le_of_finite; UndergradKB.Topology.connectedSpace_iff_torsionFree_dual; UndergradKB.Topology.torsionFree_iff_dual_divisible; UndergradKB.Topology.metrizableSpace_iff_countable_dual | depends on duality; not in Mathlib / this library: WeightDual.lean adds (i): the weight (bridging definition topWeight, least cardinality of a basis, Exercises 9.4 #14) of a compact Hausdorff abelian group equals the cardinality of its dual. Upper bound: G is closed in T^(dual) and T^iota has a basis indexed by finite subsets of iota x (countable basis of T). Lower bound: by no small subgroups of T, every character maps a basic neighbourhood W of 1 into a small ball, and for fixed W only finitely many characters do (finite cover of G by translates of W); a basis of an infinite Hausdorff space is infinite. Finite case: Dedekind independence of characters gives \|dual\| <= \|G\|, and G embeds in its double dual. (ii)-(iv) from DualityCompact2.lean. | Duality |

| TP-496 | The underlying space of an infinite totally disconnected compact group is a Cantor cube | Thm A5.0.23 @p509 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_cantorCube_of_group; UndergradKB.Topology.CantorCube.nonempty_homeomorph_pi; UndergradKB.Topology.CantorCube.exists_homeomorph_cantorCube_pi; UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_cantor_of_group; UndergradKB.Topology.CantorBrouwer.nonempty_homeomorph_cantor_of_perfect | Mathlib has profinite groups (ProfiniteGrp) but not this topological characterisation / this library: CantorCube.lean: all weights, in the book's form: an infinite totally disconnected compact (Hausdorff) group is homeomorphic to {0,1}^κ for an infinite set κ. Proof without cross-sections: well-order the open normal subgroups U_α, put N_α = intersection of the earlier U_β, and send g to the coset modulo U_α of (chosen representative of g modulo N_α U_α)⁻¹ g; this is a continuous bijection onto the product of the (finite, discrete) images of N_α in G/U_α (injective and surjective by transfinite induction, surjectivity also by compactness). Dropping one-point factors and grouping the rest into countably infinite blocks, each block is the Cantor space by Brouwer's theorem (CantorBrouwer.lean, which also keeps the metrizable form {0,1}^aleph_0). Closes a recorded loose end. | Structure of compact groups |
| TP-497 | The identity component G_0 is a closed normal subgroup, and G/G_0 is a totally disconnected group | Def A5.0.24 @p509 · Prop A5.0.25 @p509 · Prop A5.2.15 @p524 · Prop A5.2.20 @p525 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.coe_connectedComponentOfOne; UndergradKB.Topology.isClosed_connectedComponentOfOne; UndergradKB.Topology.normal_connectedComponentOfOne; UndergradKB.Topology.isConnected_fiber_quotient_connectedComponentOfOne; UndergradKB.Topology.totallyDisconnectedSpace_quotient_connectedComponentOfOne; UndergradKB.Topology.isTopologicalGroup_totallyDisconnected_quotient_connectedComponentOfOne; Subgroup.connectedComponentOfOne; mul_mem_connectedComponent_one; IsTopologicalGroup.totallyDisconnectedSpace_iff_connectedComponent_one; smul_connectedComponent | identity component is a subgroup and translation of components exist; closedness, normality and total disconnectedness of the quotient not located as a statement / this library: IdentityComponent.lean: identity component (Mathlib's Subgroup.connectedComponentOfOne) is closed and normal; G/G0 totally disconnected (via IsCoinducing.preimage_connectedComponent and connected cosets) and a topological group by Mathlib's quotient instance; compact-group clause not stated | Structure of compact groups |
| TP-498 | A compact group is homeomorphic to G_0 times G/G_0 | Thm A5.0.26 @p510 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_identityComponent_prod_of_compact; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_homeomorph_identityComponent_prod_cantorCube; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_continuous_section_of_totallyDisconnected; UndergradKB.Topology.Selection.exists_continuous_selection; UndergradKB.Topology.continuous_mk_of_map; UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_identityComponent_prod; UndergradKB.Topology.nonempty_homeomorph_identityComponent_prod_cantor | not in Mathlib / this library: CompactSection.lean: all compact (Hausdorff) groups, in the book's form: G is homeomorphic to G_0 x G/G_0, and to G_0 x {0,1}^κ (κ infinite) when G/G_0 is infinite. The cross-section of G -> G/H (H closed, G/H totally disconnected) is built by Zorn's lemma on partial sections continuous modulo a closed subgroup N; a maximal one has N = 1, since a nontrivial N is cut down by the kernel of a finite-dimensional unitary representation (Peter-Weyl, Theorem A5.10.1), the new matrix coordinate chosen by zero-dimensional selection for lower semicontinuous set-valued maps. The metrizable case of (IdentityComponentProduct.lean) is kept. | Structure of compact groups |

| TP-500 | Hilbert 5 for compact groups: Lie iff NSS iff locally Euclidean | Thm A5.0.28 @p510 | partly in Mathlib | UndergradKB.Topology.isNSS_iff_exists_isClosedEmbedding_unitary; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_isClosedEmbedding_unitary_of_isNSS; UndergradKB.Topology.isNSS_of_injective_unitary_pi; UndergradKB.Topology.isNSS_of_injective_normedRing; UndergradKB.Topology.eq_one_of_norm_pow_sub_one_le; UndergradKB.Topology.isNSS_unitary_pi; UndergradKB.Topology.IsNSS; UndergradKB.Topology.isNSS_circle | Hilbert's fifth problem not in Mathlib / this library: Hilbert's fifth problem not in Mathlib (no closed subgroup theorem for Lie groups). / this library: PeterWeylOrthogonal.lean and NSSLinear.lean: a compact Hausdorff group is NSS if and only if it is topologically isomorphic to a closed subgroup of a finite product of unitary groups U(n_i). NSS implies linear by finitely many Peter-Weyl representations with common kernel inside the NSS neighbourhood; linear implies NSS because in a real normed algebra an element all of whose powers lie within 1/2 of 1 is 1 (Cesaro averages), so every subgroup of the ball of radius 1/2 about 1 is trivial. Missing for (i) and (iii): Cartan's closed subgroup theorem (closed subgroups of U(n) are Lie groups) and, for the locally euclidean characterisation, that a locally euclidean compact group is NSS (invariance of domain). | Structure of compact groups |

| TP-549 | Closed subgroups of R^n x Z^m and of R^n x T^m x D are of the form R^a x T^b x D' (with the quotients) | Cor A5.5.17 @p547 · Cor A5.5.18 @p547 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.closedSubgroup_real_pow_prod_int_pow; UndergradKB.Topology.quotient_closedSubgroup_real_pow_prod_int_pow; UndergradKB.Topology.closedSubgroup_real_circle_discrete; UndergradKB.Topology.quotient_closedSubgroup_real_circle_discrete; UndergradKB.Topology.range_equiv_of_isOpenMap; UndergradKB.Topology.closed_subgroup_equiv_bounds; UndergradKB.Topology.subgroup_int_pow_equiv; UndergradKB.Topology.quotient_equiv_of_equiv; UndergradKB.Topology.quotient_prod_equiv; UndergradKB.Topology.ClosedSubgroupsRn.quotient_closed_subgroup_equiv | not found / this library: Corollary A5.5.17 in full (GroupsWave2i.lean, GroupsWave2k.lean, completed in): a closed subgroup of R^n x Z^m is R^a x Z^b with a <= n, a + b <= n + m; the quotient is R^c x T^d x D with c + d <= n and D discrete, generated by at most m elements. Corollary A5.5.18 (GroupsWave3.lean): a closed subgroup G of R^n x T^m x D (D discrete abelian) is R^a x T^b x D' and (R^n x T^m x D)/G is R^c x T^d x D'' with D', D'' discrete, a + b <= n + m, c + d <= n + m. Route (not the book's free resolution): rho : R^n x R^m -> R^n x T^m x D is a continuous open homomorphism; the range of such a map from a vector space with closed kernel is an open divisible subgroup R^c x T^d (Theorem A5.5.13); rho^-1(G) contains a vector subspace V open in it, rho restricted to V is open into G; the open divisible subgroup splits off with discrete complement (Proposition A5.3.8). | Subgroups of R^n |

Not in the book's generality for an arbitrary neighbourhood V and a merely continuous onto f: the extension need not agree with f on all of V and the image of the ball need not generate the same subgroup as U (the book's lemma appears too strong as stated) (ErrataWave3.lean): the book's statement for an arbitrary neighbourhood V is refuted in Lean: V = (-1,1) union [10,11] in R, f = identity on (-1,1) and x - 21/2 on [10,11] is continuous, onto (-1,1), additive whenever x, y, x+y lie in V, but has no additive extension to R (erratum; the lemma holds for a ball or any connected V, which is the form proved). | Local isomorphism |
| TP-554 | A Hausdorff abelian group locally isomorphic to R^n is isomorphic to R^a x T^b x D with a+b=n; connected case R^a x T^b | Thm A5.5.25 @p549 · Cor A5.5.26 @p549 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.exists_equiv_real_circle_discrete_of_addLocallyIsomorphic; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_equiv_real_circle_of_addLocallyIsomorphic; UndergradKB.Topology.quotient_discrete_subgroup_equiv; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_open_hom_of_addLocallyIsomorphic; UndergradKB.Topology.AddLocallyIsomorphic | not found / this library: GroupsWave2h.lean: Theorem A5.5.25: an abelian topological group (Hausdorff, though not needed) additively locally isomorphic to R^n (bridging definition AddLocallyIsomorphic, equal to LocallyIsomorphic on Multiplicative copies) is topologically isomorphic to R^a x T^b x D with D discrete and a + b = n (T = AddCircle 1); Corollary A5.5.26: connected case R^a x T^b. Route as in the book: extension of the local isomorphism on a ball is a continuous open homomorphism R^n -> G with discrete kernel, its range is an open divisible subgroup, a topological direct factor with discrete complement; the dimension count a + b = n comes from quotient_discrete_subgroup_equiv (R^n / discrete subgroup of rank q = R^(n-q) x T^q) | Local isomorphism |

| TP-579 | If G satisfies duality and nontrivial Hausdorff quotients of the dual have nontrivial characters, a subgroup of the dual separating points of G is dense. | Prop A5.9.10 @p570 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.dense_of_separatesPoints_of_dualityHolds; UndergradKB.Topology.QuotientsOfDualHaveCharacters | Technical ingredient of duality; not in Mathlib. / this library: DualityWave3.lean: in the book's generality (G an abelian topological group satisfying duality, every nontrivial Hausdorff quotient of the dual has a nontrivial character), by the book's proof: a nontrivial character of the dual modulo the closure of S is evaluation at some g different from 1, which S cannot separate from 1. | Duality theorem |
| TP-580 | Peter-Weyl: a compact Hausdorff group has enough continuous unitary representations to separate points. | Thm A5.10.1 @p572 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.exists_unitaryMatrixRep_ne_one; UndergradKB.Topology.unitaryMatrixReps_separate_points; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_unitaryRep_ne_one; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeylGeneral.exists_invariant_subspace; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeylGeneral.continuous_translRep; UndergradKB.Topology.characters_separate_points_of_compact | grep -ri 'peter.?weyl' found nothing; Mathlib has no representation theory of compact groups in this form. / this library: PeterWeylGeneral.lean, PeterWeylGeneral2.lean: the book's statement for every compact Hausdorff group: for g different from e a continuous homomorphism into the unitary matrices U(n) with value at g different from e. Spectral route, generalising 's abelian proof: on L^2 of Haar measure the operator with continuous symmetric bump kernel phi(y^-1 x) is compact, takes continuous values and commutes with left translations; A = T T* has a nonzero eigenvalue on the orthogonal complement of the g-invariant functions; its eigenspace is finite dimensional, translation invariant, made of continuous functions and moved by g; translation on it is a continuous unitary representation, written as unitary matrices in an orthonormal basis. Irreducibility (mentioned in the book's first sentence) is not stated; the abelian case n = 1 is DualityCompact.lean. | Peter-Weyl |
| TP-581 | A compact Hausdorff abelian group has enough characters to separate points. | Cor A5.10.2 @p572 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.characters_separate_points_of_compact; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_char_ne_one_of_compact; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeyl.exists_char_ne_one_of_measure; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeyl.exists_common_eigenvector; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeyl.isCompactOperator_kernelOp; UndergradKB.Topology.PeterWeyl.kernelOp_transl | Not found (grep character separat, PontryaginDual). / this library: PeterWeylAbelian.lean, PeterWeylAbelian2.lean, DualityCompact.lean: proved by the spectral route (Haar measure on K, L^2; the integral operator T with a continuous symmetric bump kernel takes values in continuous functions, is compact by Arzela-Ascoli and commutes with translations; A = T T* restricted to the orthogonal complement of the g-invariant functions is compact self-adjoint and nonzero, so it has a nonzero eigenvalue with finite-dimensional eigenspace (Mathlib's spectral theorem for compact self-adjoint operators); a common eigenvector of the commuting translations is a continuous F with F(ax) = chi(a) F(x), chi a continuous character with chi(g) different from 1). | Peter-Weyl |

Proof (Kaplansky): IsCycBasisOn S y = independent generating family of nonzero elements; cycBasis_step (if p^(k+1) S = 0 and pS has such a family, lift the generators and complete with a basis of a complement, inside the p-socle of S, of the span of the elements (ord/p) * y_i); induction on k for p-groups; splitting n = p^a * r by Bezout and strong induction on n; conversion of the family to an AddEquiv with the direct sum. | Restricted products |
| TP-594 | A compact Hausdorff abelian torsion group is a topological product of finite cyclic groups with finitely many distinct orders. | Thm A5.11.12 @p580 | proved here | UndergradKB.Topology.compact_torsion_equiv_pi_cyclic; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_pow_eq_one_of_compact_torsion; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_mulEquiv_directSum_dual_of_compact_torsion; UndergradKB.Topology.isCyclic_dual_of_isCyclic; UndergradKB.Topology.restrictedDirectProduct_congr; UndergradKB.Topology.mulEquivRestrictedDirectProduct; UndergradKB.Topology.exists_addEquiv_directSum_cyclic_of_exponent; UndergradKB.Topology.dualityHolds_of_compact; UndergradKB.Topology.dualProdEquivOfDualDiscrete; UndergradKB.Topology.dualCongr | Not found. / this library: GroupsBounded3.lean, GroupsBounded4.lean: in full (compact_torsion_equiv_pi_cyclic: a compact Hausdorff abelian torsion group G is topologically isomorphic to a product over some index type of finite cyclic groups Multiplicative (ZMod (k i)) with the discrete topology, all k i > 0, and Set.range k finite). Proof: bounded exponent N by Baire category (closed sets x^n = 1); the dual D is then of exponent N, hence a restricted direct sum of finite cyclic groups (TP-593); D is discrete (dual of a compact group), so D ~ (prod of the duals of the Z(m_i))^ topologically (dualProdEquivOfDualDiscrete with duality for the finite discrete Z(m_i)); duality for the compact groups G and Q = prod (Z(m_i))^ (dualCongr, dualityHolds_of_compact) gives G ~ Q; each (Z(m_i))^ is cyclic (injects into the circle, finite subgroups of the units of a field are cyclic); every k i divides N, so only finitely many orders occur. | Restricted products |
